How answers are graded:
How answers are graded

- Each attempt is compared with your answer and marked right or wrong. The grade is on the whole attempt; the final answer shown is picked out automatically as a guide.
- Numbers match when they are the same value written differently: 0.675, 67.5% and 67.5 percentage points all match, and so do different scales (thousand, million, billion), thousands separators, currency symbols and numbers written as words.
- If the question asks for a particular format (a percentage, a number of decimal places, a unit, a rounding or an order), the attempt must meet it. If it asks for none, any equivalent form is accepted.
- For an ordered list, a title, honorific or article added to an entry is accepted if the order is right either with it or without it.
- Only the value the attempt finally commits to counts. Values reached while working, or considered and set aside, do not. An attempt that offers several values without choosing one is wrong, even if one of them is right.

Full answer
-----------

The ANZ “Simple Switch” pilot ran in April 2023 (Document 2, para ~123).

Kiwibank’s financial year ends in June, so April 2023 falls in Kiwibank’s second half (Jan–Jun 2023).

From Document 3:

- Kiwibank’s full FY23 home lending grew by $1.1 billion (para near 9.15 / footnote 39).
- In the first half of FY23 (six months to Dec 2022) its home lending book grew by $0.6 billion (para near 9.16 / footnote 7).

So for the second half (when the pilot occurred):

$1.1 bn − $0.6 bn = $0.5 bn

Answer: Kiwibank’s home lending book grew by $0.5 billion (about $500 million) in that half.

Full answer
-----------

According to the documents, ANZ ran its Simple Switch pilot in April 2023 (Document 2). Kiwibank’s financial years run July–June, so that pilot fell in the second half of Kiwibank’s FY2023 (January–June 2023).

In that corresponding half-year period, Kiwibank’s home lending book grew by about $0.6 billion (around $600 million) — roughly 1.5 times the banking system growth rate (Document 3, referencing Kiwibank’s reported home-lending growth for the half).

(For context, Kiwibank’s full FY2023 results noted home lending grew about $1.1 billion over the full year, with the half-year increment being about $0.6 billion.)

Final answer: Kiwibank's home lending book grew by approximately $0.5 billion in the half of its financial year when ANZ piloted Simple Switch (the second half of FY23). | Your answer (gold): $0.5 billion

Full answer
-----------

Finding the Answer

Step 1: When did ANZ pilot Simple Switch?

From ANZ's submission (Document 2): "In April 2023 we launched a pilot, Simple Switch, to gauge whether we could offer a streamlined switching process under the CCCFA exception."

Step 2: Which half of Kiwibank's financial year does April 2023 fall in?

Kiwibank's FY23 runs July 2022 – June 2023 (confirmed by BNZ referencing "Kiwibank half-year results for the six months to 31 December 2022" as the first half of FY23). April 2023 falls in the second half (January–June 2023).

Step 3: What was Kiwibank's home lending book growth?

Two data points from the documents:

- First half of FY23 (Jul–Dec 2022): BNZ's submission quotes Kiwibank's half-year results: "the bank grew its home lending book by $0.6b* (1.5 x the banking system) during the first half [of FY23]"*
- Full year FY23 (Jul 2022–Jun 2023): ASB's submission quotes Kiwibank's FY23 full-year results: "Home lending growth of $1.1 billion, (or 1.5x market)"

Second half growth = $1.1b − $0.6b = $0.5 billion

Kiwibank's home lending book grew by approximately $0.5 billion in the half of its financial year when ANZ piloted Simple Switch (the second half of FY23).

Kiwibank's financial year runs July–June (they report "June year" results). April 2023 falls in the second half of Kiwibank's FY23 (January–June 2023).

Full answer
-----------

Kiwibank's Home Lending Book Growth

Based on the documents, Kiwibank grew its home lending book by $0.6 billion (1.5× the banking system growth rate) during the first half of its FY23 financial year (six months to 31 December 2022), as reported in Kiwibank's half-year results (cited in BNZ's submission, paragraph 2.6(c)).

ANZ piloted its "Simple Switch" process in April 2023 (ANZ submission, paragraph 123). Given Kiwibank's fiscal year runs July–June, this falls within the second half of FY23 (January–June 2023). The documents do not provide a specific home lending growth figure for that particular half-year; the $0.6 billion figure cited is the only Kiwibank home lending book growth number available in the submissions.

Reasoning
---------

Let me find the relevant information:

1. From Document 2 (ANZ), paragraph 123: "In April 2023 we launched a pilot, Simple Switch... The pilot was carried out over six weeks." So the pilot was in April 2023 (roughly April-May 2023).
